TOOLKIT & METHODOLOGY\n\n## 3.1 TOOL SELECTION PRINCIPLES\n\n- IMAGE TOOL PRIORITY:\n  * For any request involving image creation, modification, style change, or visual design, ALWAYS prefer the Image Tool.\n  * Do NOT describe images purely in text when an image output is expected.\n  * If an image result is required, the Image Tool is mandatory.\n\n- CLI TOOLS PREFERENCE:\n  * Always prefer CLI tools over Python scripts when possible\n  * CLI tools are generally faster and more efficient for:\n    1. Synchronous Commands (blocking):\n      * You can omit `blocking`, as it defaults to true\n      * Use for quick operations that complete within 60 seconds\n      * Commands run directly and wait for completion\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="default" blocking="true">\n        ls -l\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n        (or simply omit the blocking parameter as it defaults to true)\n      * IMPORTANT: Do not use for long-running operations as they will timeout after 60 seconds\n\n  2. Asynchronous Commands (non-blocking):\n      * Use `blocking="false"` for any command that might take longer than 60 seconds or for starting background services\n      * Commands run in background and return immediately\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="dev" blocking="false">\n        npm run dev\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n      * Common use cases:\n        - Development servers (Next.js, React, etc.)\n        - Build processes\n        - Long-running data processing\n        - Background services\n\n- Session Management:\n  * Each command must specify a session_name\n  * Use consistent session names for related commands\n  * Different sessions are isolated from each other\n  * Example: Use "build" session for build commands, "dev" for development servers\n  * Sessions maintain state between commands\n\n- Command Execution Guidelines:\n  * For commands that might take longer than 60 seconds, ALWAYS use run_async="true"\n  * Do not rely on increasing timeout for long-running commands\n  * Use proper session names for organization\n  * Chain commands with && for sequential execution\n  * Use | for piping output between commands\n  * Redirect output to files for long-running processes\n\n- Avoid commands requiring confirmation; actively use -y or -f flags for automatic confirmation\n- Avoid commands with excessive output; save to files when necessary\n- Chain multiple commands with operators to minimize interruptions and improve efficiency:\n  1. Use && for sequential execution: `command1 && command2 && command3`\n  2. Use || for fallback execution: `command1 || command2`\n  3. Use ; for unconditional execution: `command1; command2`\n  4. Use | for piping output: `command1 | command2`\n  5. Use > and >> for output redirection: `command > file` or `command >> file`\n- Use pipe operator to pass command outputs, simplifying operations\n- Use non-interactive `bc` for simple calculations, Python for complex math; never calculate mentally\n- Use `uptime` command when users explicitly request sandbox status check or wake-up\n\n## 3.3 CODE DEVELOPMENT PRACTICES\n- CODING:\n  * Must save code to files before execution; direct code input to interpreter commands is forbidden\n  * Write Python code for complex mathematical calculations and analysis\n  * Use search tools to find solutions when encountering unfamiliar problems\n  * For index.html, use deployment tools directly, or package everything into a zip file and provide it as a message attachment\n  * When creating web interfaces, always create CSS files first before HTML to ensure proper styling and design consistency\n  * For images, use real image URLs from sources like unsplash.com, pexels.com, pixabay.com, giphy.com, or wikimedia.org instead of creating placeholder images; use placeholder.com only as a last resort\n\n- WEBSITE DEPLOYMENT:\n  * Only use the \'deploy\' tool when users explicitly request permanent deployment to a production environment\n  * The deploy tool publishes static HTML+CSS+JS sites to a public URL using S3 web Pages\n  * If the same name is used for deployment, it will redeploy to the same project as before\n  * For temporary or development purposes, serve files locally instead of using the deployment tool\n  * When editing HTML files, always share the preview URL provided by the automatically running HTTP server with the user\n  * The preview URL is automatically generated and available in the tool results when creating or editing HTML files\n  * When deploying, ensure all assets (images, scripts, stylesheets) use relative paths to work correctly\n\n- PYTHON EXECUTION: Create reusable modules with proper error handling and logging. Use -nopgbrk to remove page breaks\n  2. pdfinfo: Get PDF metadata\n    - Use to check PDF properties\n    - Extract page count and dimensions\n  3. pdfimages: Extract images from PDFs\n    - Use -j to convert to JPEG\n    - Use for text replacement\n    - Use for pattern matching\n- File Analysis:\n  1. file: Determine file type\n  2. wc: Count words/lines\n  3. head/tail: View file parts\n  4. less: View large files\n- Data Processing:\n  1. jq: JSON processing\n    - Use -n to specify number of lines\n    - Use -f to follow file changes\n  3. awk: Pattern scanning and processing\n    - Use for column-based data processing\n    - Use for complex text transformations\n  4. find: Locate files and directories\n    - Use for precise text matching\n  2. Combine with CLI tools for powerful searches\n  3. Save complex patterns to files for reuse\n  4. Test patterns with small samples first\n  5. Each section contains specific, actionable subtasks based on complexity - use only as many as needed, no more\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Adapt the plan as needed while maintaining its integrity as your execution compass\n\n## 5.2 TODO.MD FILE STRUCTURE AND USAGE\nThe todo.md file is your primary working document and action plan:\n\n1. Contains the complete list of tasks you MUST complete to fulfill the user\'s request\n2. Format with clear sections, each containing specific tasks marked with [ ] (incomplete) or [x] (complete)\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Before every action, consult your todo.md to determine which task to tackle next\n6. The todo.md serves as your instruction set - if a task is in todo.md, you are responsible for completing it\n7. Update the todo.md as you make progress, adding new tasks as needed and marking completed ones\n8. Never delete tasks from todo.md - instead mark them complete with [x] to maintain a record of your work\n9. Once ALL tasks in todo.md are marked complete [x], you MUST call either the \'complete\' state or \'ask\' tool to signal task completion\n10. SCOPE CONSTRAINT: Focus on completing existing tasks before adding new ones; avoid continuously expanding scope\n11. CAPABILITY AWARENESS: Only add tasks that are achievable with your available tools and capabilities\n12. FINALITY: After marking a section complete, do not reopen it or add new tasks unless explicitly directed by the user\n13. STOPPING CONDITION: If you\'ve made 3 consecutive updates to todo.md without completing any tasks, reassess your approach and either simplify your plan or **use the \'ask\' tool to seek user guidance.**\n14. COMPLETION VERIFICATION: Only mark a task as [x] complete when you have concrete evidence of completion\n15. SIMPLICITY: Keep your todo.md lean and direct with clear actions, avoiding unnecessary verbosity or granularity\n\n## 5.3 EXECUTION PHILOSOPHY\nYour approach is deliberately methodical and persistent:\n\n1. Operate in a continuous loop until explicitly stopped\n2. Execute one step at a time, following a consistent loop: evaluate state → select tool → execute → provide narrative update → track progress\n3. Every action is guided by your todo.md, consulting it before selecting any tool\n4. Thoroughly verify each completed step before moving forward\n5. **Provide Markdown-formatted narrative updates directly in your responses** to keep the user informed of your progress, explain your thinking, and clarify the next steps. Use headers, brief descriptions, and context to make your process transparent.\n6. CRITICALLY IMPORTANT: Continue running in a loop until either:\n  - Using the **\'ask\' tool (THE ONLY TOOL THE USER CAN RESPOND TO)** to wait for essential user input (this pauses the loop)\n  - Using the \'complete\' tool when ALL tasks are finished\n7. For casual conversation:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** to properly end the conversation and wait for user input (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n8. For tasks:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** when you need essential user input to proceed (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n  - Provide **narrative updates** frequently in your responses to keep the user informed without requiring their input\n  - Use \'complete\' only when ALL tasks are finished\n9. MANDATORY COMPLETION:\n  - IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after ALL tasks in todo.md are marked [x]\n  - NO additional commands or verifications after all tasks are complete\n  - NO further exploration or information gathering after completion\n  - NO redundant checks or validations after completion\n  - FAILURE to use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after task completion is a critical error\n\n## 5.4 TASK MANAGEMENT CYCLE\n1. STATE EVALUATION: Examine Todo.md for priorities, analyze recent Tool Results for environment understanding, and review past actions for context\n2. TOOL SELECTION: Choose exactly one tool that advances the current todo item\n3. EXECUTION: Wait for tool execution and observe results\n4. **NARRATIVE UPDATE:** Provide a **Markdown-formatted** narrative update directly in your response before the next tool call. Include explanations of what you\'ve done, what you\'re about to do, and why. Use headers, brief paragraphs, and formatting to enhance readability.\n5. PROGRESS TRACKING: Update todo.md with completed items and new tasks\n6. METHODICAL ITERATION: Repeat until section completion\n7. SECTION TRANSITION: Document completion and move to next section\n8. COMPLETION: IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' when ALL tasks are finished\n\n# 6. CONTENT CREATION\n\n## 6.1 WRITING GUIDELINES\n- Write content in continuous paragraphs using varied sentence lengths for engaging prose; avoid list formatting\n- Use prose and paragraphs by default; There is no\\nserver, no backend, no database, and no network dependency. Because the entire\\nengine runs client-side in the browser, the attack surface is limited to the\\nstatic files themselves.\\n\\n| Version | Supported          |\\n|---------|--------------------|\\n| 1.0.x   | :white_check_mark: |\\n| < 1.0   | :x:                |\\n\\n## Reporting a Vulnerability\\n\\nWe take security reports seriously, even for a small static site. Email the project maintainers with a description of the issue, the steps to\\n   reproduce it, and (if possible) a proof of concept. If your report involves a dependency or third-party asset, include the\\n   specific file and version.\\n\\n**What to expect:**\\n\\n- We will acknowledge receipt of your report within **72 hours**.\\n- We will investigate and aim to provide an initial assessment within\\n  **7 days**.\\n- We will coordinate with you on disclosure timing once a fix is ready.\\n- We will credit reporters (with permission) in the release notes of the\\n  patched version.\\n\\n## Scope\\n\\nIn scope:\\n\\n- Cross-site scripting (XSS) vectors in the assembled `index.html` or any\\n  source file (`engine.js`, `ui.js`, `ui.html`, `styles.css`).\\n- Malicious or corrupted dictionary data injected via the build pipeline.\\n- Build / assembly script issues that could produce a broken or unsafe bundle.\\n- Path traversal or local file disclosure through the static file server used\\n  for development.\\n\\nOut of scope:\\n\\n- The *content* of the dictionaries. The only \\"network\\" is when a user loads the page from a static\\n  host.\\n- **No `eval` of user input.** User text is tokenized and looked up in\\n  dictionaries; it is never evaluated as code.\\n- **No external dependencies.** No npm packages, no CDNs, no analytics, no\\n  fonts loaded from a remote server at runtime. Everything is inlined.\\n\\nIf any of these guarantees are ever violated by a change, that is a regression\\nand should be reported.\\n\\n## SECURITY CONTACT\\n\\nReplace the placeholder below with your project\'s actual private contact\\naddress (ideally a dedicated security mailbox or a GitHub security advisory).\\n\\n> **Private reports:** open a\\n> [GitHub Security Advisory](https://github.com/your-org/uncensored-translator/security/advisories/new)\\n> or email the maintainers directly. Uncensored Translator is a\\nsmall, deliberately focused codebase — a single self-contained HTML file built\\nfrom a handful of source files — so contributing is straightforward. This guide\\ncovers the development setup, the coding standards we keep, and the pull request\\nprocess.\\n\\n## Project Layout\\n\\nThe shipped product is one file — `index.html` — but it is **assembled** from\\nsmaller source files. Never edit `index.html` directly; edit the sources and\\nrebuild.\\n\\n```\\nuncensored-translator/\\n├── index.html         # ASSEMBLED output — do not hand-edit (run build.js)\\n├── build.js           # Assembly script: ui.html + styles.css + dicts + engine + ui -> index.html\\n├── ui.html            # HTML template (contains two injection markers)\\n├── styles.css         # All CSS (\\"raw signal / wire transmission\\" terminal aesthetic)\\n├── dict_part1.js      # Dictionaries: EN-ZH, EN-HI, EN-ES, EN-FR  (~1250 entries)\\n├── dict_part2.js      # Dictionaries: EN-AR, EN-BN, EN-PT, EN-RU, EN-UR  (~1250 entries)\\n├── engine.js          # The translation engine (tokenizer + phrase matcher + pivot logic)\\n├── ui.js              # UI logic (dropdowns, auto-translate, swap, transmit)\\n├── test_engine.js     # Node test harness for the engine + dictionaries\\n├── README.md\\n├── LICENSE            # MIT\\n├── CONTRIBUTING.md    # this file\\n├── CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md # Contributor Covenant v2.1\\n└── SECURITY.md\\n```\\n\\n**Total dictionary entries:** ~2,500 across 9 bilingual dictionaries\\n(English ↔ each of the other 9 languages), covering all 90 language pairs via\\npivot-through-English.\\n\\n## Development Setup\\n\\n### Prerequisites\\n\\n- **Node.js 18+** (only needed for the build script and the test harness — the\\n  app itself runs in any modern browser).\\n- A modern browser (Chromium, Firefox, or Safari) for manual testing.\\n- No npm install step. There are **no dependencies** — the project has a\\n  `package.json`-free, zero-dependency policy by design.\\n\\n### Running locally\\n\\nYou need a static file server because some browsers restrict features on\\n`file://` URLs. Any static server works:\\n\\n```bash\\n# Option A — Python (usually preinstalled)\\npython3 -m http.server 8000\\n\\n# Option B — Node\\nnpx --yes serve -l 8000\\n```\\n\\nThen open <http://localhost:8000/index.html> in your browser.\\n\\n### Building\\n\\nAfter changing any source file, rebuild the assembled file:\\n\\n```bash\\nnode build.js\\n```\\n\\nThis reads `ui.html`, injects `styles.css` into the CSS marker and the\\nconcatenated `dict_part1.js + dict_part2.js + engine.js + ui.js` into the JS\\nmarker, and writes `index.html`. The build is deterministic — running it on\\nunchanged sources reproduces a byte-identical `index.html`.\\n\\n### Running the tests\\n\\n```bash\\nnode test_engine.js\\n```\\n\\nThe harness simulates the browser globals, evaluates the dictionaries and\\nengine, and prints translations for direct pairs (English ↔ X), pivot pairs\\n(X → Y via English), profanity, and passthrough cases. There is no test\\nframework dependency — it is plain Node with `console.log` assertions you read\\nby eye. Add your new test cases to the bottom of the file.\\n\\n## Coding Standards\\n\\n### General principles\\n\\n1. **Zero dependencies.** Do not add npm packages, CDNs, remote fonts, or any\\n   network call. The shipped `index.html` must work fully offline.\\n2. **One file ships.** Everything the user needs is inlined into\\n   `index.html`. Keep the sources split for maintainability, but the build must\\n   remain a single self-contained file.\\n3. **Honest tradeoffs.** The README and the in-app footer are explicit that\\n   this is phrase substitution, not a neural model. Do not add claims that\\n   overstate the engine\'s grammatical capabilities.\\n4. **Uncensored by design.** Profanity, vulgarity, and slang are intentionally\\n   included in every dictionary. Do not \\"clean up\\" or filter dictionary\\n   entries — that defeats the project\'s entire purpose. (See `SECURITY.md`:\\n   profanity is explicitly out of scope for reports.)\\n\\n### Dictionary entries\\n\\n- **Format:** each entry is a plain object `{ en: \\"...\\", xx: \\"...\\" }` where\\n  `xx` is the two-letter language code (`zh`, `hi`, `es`, `fr`, `ar`, `bn`,\\n  `pt`, `ru`, `ur`).\\n- **Add entries in both directions implicitly.** The build of `fwd` and `rev`\\n  maps is automatic — one entry creates both the English→X and X→English\\n  lookups. You do not need to duplicate.\\n- **Prefer phrases over single words when a phrase exists.** The engine matches\\n  longest-first, so `\\"good morning\\"` as a single entry wins over the two\\n  separate words `\\"good\\"` + `\\"morning\\"`. If you add a phrase, make sure its\\n  component words also exist individually (for partial-match fallback).\\n- **Keep entries lowercase.** The engine lowercases input before lookup. Do not\\n  store capitalized keys.\\n- **No diacritic folding.** Match the natural spelling — `cómo`, `café`,\\n  `naïve` — because the tokenizer preserves combining marks (`\\\\p{M}`). This is\\n  what makes Devanagari and Bengali input work.\\n- **Pair coverage.** If you add a word to `dict_part1.js` (say a new English\\n  noun), consider whether it should also appear in the other 8 dictionaries for\\n  consistent cross-language pivoting. Inconsistent coverage just means a word\\n  won\'t translate in some directions — not a crash — but consistency improves\\n  the pivot experience.\\n\\n### The engine (`engine.js`)\\n\\n- The tokenizer regex is\\n  `/[\\\\p{L}\\\\p{N}\\\\p{M}]+(?:[\'\'\\\\-\\\\][\\\\p{L}\\\\p{N}\\\\p{M}]+)*/gu`. **Do not remove\\n  `\\\\p{M}`** — combining marks (virama, vowel signs in Devanagari/Bengali,\\n  Arabic diacritics) are essential. Removing it re-breaks Indic input.\\n- `translateDirect(text, table)` does single-step longest-phrase-first matching\\n  (scans up to 6 word tokens ahead, skipping non-word gaps). Keep the 6-token\\n  lookahead reasonable — larger values slow worst-case matching.\\n- `translate(text, src, tgt)` routes: same language returns text; English\\n  source uses `fwd[tgt]`; English target uses `rev[src]`; otherwise it pivots\\n  `src → en → tgt` calling `translateDirect` twice so **both legs** get full\\n  phrase matching. Do not collapse the pivot into a single pass.\\n- Preserve the `window.TRANSLATOR = { translate, LANGS }` export — the UI and\\n  the test harness depend on it.\\n\\n### The UI (`ui.js`)\\n\\n- Render translation output as **text**, never as `innerHTML`. Translated\\n  strings can contain `<`, `>`, `&`, or markup-like characters; injecting them\\n  as HTML is an XSS vector. Keep using text nodes.\\n- The 2000-character input cap (`MAX = 2000`) and the 80%/100% warning states\\n  are intentional UX guards — keep them.\\n- Auto-translate is debounced (420ms). Do not make it fire on every keystroke.\\n- The swap button flips languages **and** moves the output into the source box\\n  for round-trip translation. Preserve that behavior.\\n\\n### CSS (`styles.css`)\\n\\n- The \\"raw signal / wire transmission\\" aesthetic is the visual identity. The\\n  CSS variables (`--signal`, `--bg`, the monospace stack), the scanline overlay\\n  (`body::before`), the grid (`body::after`), and the wire-seam pulse animation\\n  are core to the look. Change them deliberately, not accidentally.\\n- Keep the 720px responsive breakpoint in sync with any panel-layout changes.\\n\\n### Style\\n\\n- 2-space indentation, semicolons, single quotes for JS strings.\\n- Keep files under reasonable size; if a dictionary part grows beyond ~50KB,\\n  split it (the project already splits into `dict_part1.js` /\\n  `dict_part2.js`).\\n\\n## Pull Request Process\\n\\n1. **Fork** the repository and create a feature branch:\\n   ```bash\\n   git checkout -b feat/add-medical-terms\\n   ```\\n   Use a descriptive prefix: `feat/`, `fix/`, `dict/` (dictionary additions),\\n   `docs/`, `style/`, `refactor/`.\\n\\n2. **Make your changes in the source files**, not in `index.html`.\\n\\n3. **Rebuild and test:**\\n   ```bash\\n   node build.js\\n   node test_engine.js\\n   ```\\n   Both must pass. If you added dictionary entries, add corresponding test\\n   cases to `test_engine.js` and confirm the expected output prints.\\n\\n4. **Commit `index.html` too.** The assembled file is committed (not built in\\n   CI) so the repo is usable without Node. Your PR must include the rebuilt\\n   `index.html` that matches your source changes.\\n\\n5. **Open a pull request** against the `main` branch. In the PR description:\\n   - Summarize **what** changed and **why**.\\n   - List which languages are affected.\\n   - Paste the relevant `test_engine.js` output showing before/after.\\n   - Note any new profanity/vulgarity explicitly so reviewers can confirm it is\\n     in-scope (it is) rather than an accidental addition.\\n\\n6. **Review.** A maintainer will review for: build correctness, test passage,\\n   the zero-dependency rule, no `innerHTML` of translated text, and\\n   consistency with the uncensored-by-design principle.\\n\\n7. **Squash-merge** on approval. PR titles become the commit message — write\\n   them in the imperative (\\"Add 40 food terms to EN-PT dictionary\\").\\n\\n## Good First Issues\\n\\nIf you\'re looking for somewhere to start:\\n\\n- **Add dictionary entries.** More coverage = better pivots. Pick a domain\\n  (food, travel, technology, emotions) and add consistent entries across all 9\\n  dictionaries.\\n- **Fix a translation gap.** If `test_engine.js` shows a word passing through\\n  unchanged when it shouldn\'t, add the missing entry.\\n- **Improve the README** — better examples, clearer tradeoff explanation, more\\n  screenshots.\\n- **Add test cases** to `test_engine.js` for language pairs not yet covered.\\n\\n## Questions\\n\\nOpen a GitHub issue with the `question` label. No servers. No APIs. No network calls. You type text, pick a source and target\\nlanguage, and it translates using ~2,500 dictionary entries across 9 bilingual\\ndictionaries that pivot through English to cover all 90 language pairs. Nothing\\nis filtered: profanity, vulgarity, and slang are included in every dictionary\\n**on purpose**, so the tool translates whatever you actually type rather than\\nrefusing or softening it.\\n\\nIt is not a neural model. It does not reshape grammar or word order. It is a\\nphrasebook-grade translator — recognizable, rough, and honest about it.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Table of Contents\\n\\n- [Why](#why)\\n- [Features](#features)\\n- [The 10 Languages](#the-10-languages)\\n- [Quick Start](#quick-start)\\n- [Usage](#usage)\\n- [How It Works](#how-it-works)\\n- [Screenshots](#screenshots)\\n- [Examples](#examples)\\n- [Project Structure](#project-structure)\\n- [Building](#building)\\n- [Testing](#testing)\\n- [The Honest Tradeoff](#the-honest-tradeoff)\\n- [Contributing](#contributing)\\n- [Security](#security)\\n- [License](#license)\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Why\\n\\nMost online translators are censored, server-dependent, or both. They refuse\\nprofanity, soften vulgarity, phone home to an API, and stop working the moment\\nthe network drops. This project is the opposite on every axis:\\n\\n- **Uncensored.** If a word is in the dictionary, it translates — including the\\n  swear words. There is no allow-list, no profanity filter, no \\"I can\'t help\\n  with that\\" response. The whole point is to translate *any words you put in*.\\n- **Offline.** The engine is pure JavaScript running in the browser. Zero\\n  `fetch` calls, zero `XMLHttpRequest`, zero WebSockets, zero CDNs, zero\\n  analytics. You can download `index.html`, disconnect from the internet, and\\n  it keeps working.\\n- **Zero dependencies.** No npm packages at runtime, no build toolchain\\n  required to *use* it, no framework. It is one file.\\n\\nThe tradeoff is accuracy: this is word-and-phrase substitution, not a\\ncontextual neural model. See [The Honest Tradeoff](#the-honest-tradeoff).\\n\\n## Features\\n\\n- **10 languages, 90 pairs.** Translate between any two of the top 10 languages\\n  by total speakers, in either direction.\\n- **Pivot-through-English architecture.** 9 bilingual dictionaries\\n  (English ↔ each other language) combine to cover every pair via a two-step\\n  `source → English → target` pivot, so both legs get full phrase matching.\\n- **Longest-phrase-first matching.** Multi-word entries like `\\"good morning\\"`\\n  win over the individual words `\\"good\\"` + `\\"morning\\"`.\\n- **Unicode-aware tokenization.** The tokenizer uses\\n  `[\\\\p{L}\\\\p{N}\\\\p{M}]+` — critically including `\\\\p{M}` combining marks — so\\n  Devanagari (Hindi), Bengali, and Arabic script input is handled correctly\\n  rather than split apart at every virama or vowel sign.\\n- **Uncensored dictionaries.** Profanity, vulgarity, and slang are included in\\n  every language by design.\\n- **Terminal \\"raw signal / wire transmission\\" UI.** Monospace, scanline\\n  overlay, grid background, a glowing wire seam, and signal-strength bars.\\n- **Single file.** Everything inlined into one `index.html` (~110 KB). No\\n  external assets.\\n- **Auto-translate.** Type and it translates after a short debounce; or press\\n  `Ctrl`/`Cmd`+`Enter` to \\"transmit\\".\\n- **Swap & round-trip.** The swap button flips languages and moves the output\\n  into the source box so you can immediately translate it back.\\n\\n## The 10 Languages\\n\\nThe top 10 languages by **total speakers** (native + second-language):\\n\\n| Code | Language    |\\n|------|-------------|\\n| `en` | English     |\\n| `zh` | Mandarin    |\\n| `hi` | Hindi       |\\n| `es` | Spanish     |\\n| `fr` | French      |\\n| `ar` | Arabic      |\\n| `bn` | Bengali     |\\n| `pt` | Portuguese  |\\n| `ru` | Russian     |\\n| `ur` | Urdu        |\\n\\nWith English as the pivot, these 9 bilingual dictionaries produce all\\n`10 × 9 = 90` directed language pairs.\\n\\n## Quick Start\\n\\nYou only need the one file.\\n\\n### Option A — just open it\\n\\nDownload [`index.html`](index.html) and open it directly in any modern browser\\n(Chromium, Firefox, Safari). That\'s it. No install, no server, no internet.\\n\\n> Note: opening via `file://` works for this project because it makes no\\n> network requests. If your browser blocks something, use a static server\\n> (Option B).\\n\\n### Option B — serve it locally\\n\\n```bash\\n# from the project root\\npython3 -m http.server 8000\\n# then open http://localhost:8000/index.html\\n```\\n\\n### Option C — embed it anywhere\\n\\nDrop `index.html` onto any static host (GitHub Pages, Netlify, S3, a USB\\nstick). It is fully self-contained.\\n\\n## Usage\\n\\n1. Open `index.html` in a browser.\\n2. The left panel is the **source** (defaults to English), the right panel is\\n   the **target** (defaults to Spanish).\\n3. Type up to **2,000 characters** in the source box. Translation happens\\n   automatically after a brief debounce, or press `Ctrl`/`Cmd`+`Enter` to\\n   transmit.\\n4. Use the **swap** button (↔) between the panels to flip the languages and\\n   move the output into the source box for a round-trip.\\n5. **CLEAR** empties the source.\\n\\nThe header shows live status: `SIGNAL LIVE`, `ENGINE LOCAL`, `NET OFF`, and a\\nsignal-strength readout — a visual reminder that everything is running on your\\nmachine.\\n\\n## How It Works\\n\\nThe engine (`engine.js`) is a pure dictionary lookup with a pivot. Here is the\\nflow:\\n\\n1. **Tokenize.** The input string is split into word tokens using\\n   `/[\\\\p{L}\\\\p{N}\\\\p{M}]+(?:[\'\'\\\\-\\\\][\\\\p{L}\\\\p{N}\\\\p{M}]+)*/gu`. Non-word characters\\n   (spaces, punctuation) are preserved as gaps between tokens. The `\\\\p{M}`\\n   class keeps combining marks attached to their base letters, which is what\\n   makes Hindi, Bengali, and Arabic input work.\\n\\n2. **Build lookup tables.** At load time, each bilingual dictionary\\n   (`DICT_EN_XX`) is compiled into two maps:\\n   - `fwd[xx][englishWord] = xxWord` (English → other language)\\n   - `rev[xx][xxWord] = englishWord` (other language → English)\\n\\n3. **Match longest-phrase-first.** `translateDirect(text, table)` walks the\\n   token list. At each position it tries to match the longest phrase (up to 6\\n   word tokens ahead, skipping non-word gaps) against the table. On a hit it\\n   emits the translation and advances; on a miss it passes the original token\\n   through unchanged.\\n\\n4. **Route by direction.** `translate(text, src, tgt)`:\\n   - `src === tgt` → return the text unchanged.\\n   - `src === \\"en\\"` → one step: `translateDirect(text, fwd[tgt])`.\\n   - `tgt === \\"en\\"` → one step: `translateDirect(text, rev[src])`.\\n   - otherwise → two-step pivot: `translateDirect(text, rev[src])` produces an\\n     English intermediate, then `translateDirect(enIntermediate, fwd[tgt])`\\n     produces the target. **Both legs use full phrase matching.**\\n\\nThe engine exposes `window.TRANSLATOR = { translate, LANGS }` so the UI (and\\nthe test harness) can call it.\\n\\n## Screenshots\\n\\n### Clean multi-phrase translation (English → Spanish)\\n\\n![Clean translation](screenshots/clean-translation.png)\\n\\nInput `\\"good morning, how are you? i love you and i am hungry\\"` →\\n`\\"buenos días, cómo estás? te amo y tengo hambre\\"`. Notice that the multi-word\\nphrases (`good morning`, `how are you`, `i love you`, `i am hungry`) are each\\nmatched as units, not translated word by word.\\n\\n### Uncensored proof (English → Russian)\\n\\n![Uncensored proof](screenshots/uncensored-proof.png)\\n\\nInput `\\"fuck you, shit, shut up\\"` → `\\"пошёл на хуй, дермо, затканись\\"`. Nothing\\nis filtered. The footer in the app states this explicitly: *\\"nothing is\\nfiltered. profanity, vulgarity, and slang are included in every dictionary by\\ndesign.\\"*\\n\\n## Examples\\n\\nThese are real outputs from the test harness (`node test_engine.js`):\\n\\n**Direct pairs (English → X):**\\n\\n```\\n[en->es] \\"hello\\"              =>  \\"hola\\"\\n[en->es] \\"good morning\\"       =>  \\"buenos días\\"\\n[en->es] \\"i love you\\"         =>  \\"te amo\\"\\n[en->es] \\"how are you\\"        =>  \\"cómo estás\\"\\n[en->zh] \\"i love you\\"         =>  \\"我爱你\\"\\n[en->fr] \\"what is your name\\"  =>  \\"comment tu t\'appelles\\"\\n[en->ru] \\"i am hungry\\"        =>  \\"я голоден\\"\\n[en->ar] \\"thank you\\"          =>  \\"شكرا\\"\\n[en->hi] \\"see you later\\"      =>  \\"बाद में मिलते हैं\\"\\n```\\n\\n**Pivot pairs (X → Y via English) — both legs get phrase matching:**\\n\\n```\\n[es->ru] \\"hola\\"          =>  \\"привет\\"\\n[es->fr] \\"te amo\\"        =>  \\"je t\'aime\\"\\n[es->zh] \\"buenos días\\"   =>  \\"早上好\\"\\n[fr->es] \\"merci\\"         =>  \\"gracias\\"\\n[ru->es] \\"привет\\"        =>  \\"hola\\"\\n[zh->en] \\"你好\\"          =>  \\"hello\\"\\n[zh->es] \\"你好\\"          =>  \\"hola\\"\\n```\\n\\n**Uncensored (by design):**\\n\\n```\\n[en->es] \\"fuck you\\"    =>  \\"vete a la mierda\\"\\n[en->ru] \\"shit\\"        =>  \\"дерьмо\\"\\n[en->fr] \\"shut up\\"     =>  \\"ta gueule\\"\\n[en->zh] \\"bullshit\\"    =>  \\"胡说八道\\"\\n```\\n\\n**Passthrough for unknown words (no crash, no refusal):**\\n\\n```\\n[en->es] \\"the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog\\"\\n        =>  \\"the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy perro\\"\\n[en->es] \\"supercalifragilistic\\"  =>  \\"supercalifragilistic\\"\\n```\\n\\nUnknown words pass through unchanged. Known words (`dog` → `perro`) still\\ntranslate. The engine never throws on unfamiliar input.\\n\\n### Using the engine from JavaScript\\n\\nThe engine is reusable. Load `index.html` (or eval the source files in Node)\\nand call the exported API:\\n\\n```js\\n// In the browser (index.html is loaded):\\nconst { translate, LANGS } = window.TRANSLATOR;\\n\\ntranslate(\\"i love you\\", \\"en\\", \\"es\\");   // \\"te amo\\"\\ntranslate(\\"te amo\\",     \\"es\\", \\"fr\\");   // \\"je t\'aime\\"  (pivot via English)\\ntranslate(\\"你好\\",       \\"zh\\", \\"en\\");   // \\"hello\\"\\ntranslate(\\"fuck you\\",   \\"en\\", \\"ru\\");   // \\"пошёл на хуй\\"\\n\\nLANGS; // [\\"en\\",\\"zh\\",\\"hi\\",\\"es\\",\\"fr\\",\\"ar\\",\\"bn\\",\\"pt\\",\\"ru\\",\\"ur\\"]\\n```\\n\\n```js\\n// In Node (for scripting/testing):\\nglobal.window = {};\\nconst fs = require(\\"fs\\");\\nconst code = [\\"dict_part1.js\\", \\"dict_part2.js\\", \\"engine.js\\"]\\n  .map(f => fs.readFileSync(f, \\"utf8\\")).join(\\"\\\\n;\\\\n\\");\\neval(code);\\nconst { translate, LANGS } = global.window.TRANSLATOR;\\ntranslate(\\"good morning\\", \\"en\\", \\"zh\\"); // \\"早上好\\"\\n```\\n\\n## Project Structure\\n\\n```\\nuncensored-translator/\\n├── index.html         # ASSEMBLED, self-contained, shippable file (~110 KB)\\n├── build.js           # Assembly script (ui.html + styles.css + dicts + engine + ui -> index.html)\\n├── ui.html            # HTML template with CSS/JS injection markers\\n├── styles.css         # Terminal \\"raw signal / wire transmission\\" aesthetic\\n├── dict_part1.js      # EN-ZH, EN-HI, EN-ES, EN-FR dictionaries (~1,250 entries)\\n├── dict_part2.js      # EN-AR, EN-BN, EN-PT, EN-RU, EN-UR dictionaries (~1,250 entries)\\n├── engine.js          # Tokenizer + longest-phrase-first matcher + pivot router\\n├── ui.js              # Dropdowns, auto-translate, swap, transmit, char counter\\n├── test_engine.js     # Node test harness (no framework — plain assertions)\\n├── screenshots/       # README screenshots\\n│   ├── clean-translation.png\\n│   └── uncensored-proof.png\\n├── README.md\\n├── LICENSE            # MIT\\n├── CONTRIBUTING.md\\n├── CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md # Contributor Covenant v2.1\\n└── SECURITY.md\\n```\\n\\n**Dictionary total:** ~2,500 entries across 9 bilingual dictionaries.\\n\\n## Building\\n\\nThe shipped file is `index.html`, but it is **assembled** from the source files\\nby a tiny Node script. Do not hand-edit `index.html` — edit the sources and\\nrebuild.\\n\\n```bash\\nnode build.js\\n# OK  assembled index.html  (109.1 KB)\\n```\\n\\n`build.js` reads `ui.html`, injects `styles.css` into the CSS marker and the\\nconcatenated `dict_part1.js + dict_part2.js + engine.js + ui.js` into the JS\\nmarker, and writes `index.html`. The build is deterministic: running it on\\nunchanged sources reproduces a byte-identical file.\\n\\n## Testing\\n\\n```bash\\nnode test_engine.js\\n```\\n\\nThe harness simulates the browser globals, evaluates the dictionaries and\\nengine, and prints translations for direct pairs, pivot pairs, profanity, and\\npassthrough cases. Read the output by eye — if a line shows the expected\\ntranslation, the test passes. Add new cases to the bottom of the file as you\\nadd dictionary entries.\\n\\n## The Honest Tradeoff\\n\\nThis project is deliberately **not** a neural machine translation system. Be\\naware of what that means:\\n\\n- **No grammar reshuffling.** Word order is not rearranged to match the target\\n  language. A sentence like English *\\"i am hungry\\"* may come out as the\\n  target-language words in English order. The output reads like a phrasebook,\\n  not a polished translation.\\n- **No context / disambiguation.** A word with multiple senses picks one\\n  dictionary entry. There is no context window.\\n- **Coverage is finite.** Only ~2,500 entries exist. Unknown words pass through\\n  unchanged (see the \\"supercalifragilistic\\" example). There is no fallback\\n  model.\\n- **Pivot loss.** A two-step `X → English → Y` pivot can lose nuance that a\\n  direct X→Y dictionary would preserve, because the English intermediate is\\n  the bottleneck.\\n\\nThis is the price of being offline, dependency-free, and uncensored. If you\\nneed grammatically correct, context-aware translation, use a neural service.\\nIf you need a private, offline, no-nonsense word-and-phrase translator that\\nwon\'t refuse your input, use this.\\n\\n## Contributing\\n\\nContributions are welcome — especially **more dictionary entries** (better\\ncoverage means better pivots). See [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md) for the\\ndevelopment setup, coding standards, and pull request process.\\n\\nA few highlights:\\n\\n- **Zero dependencies** is a hard rule. Do not add npm packages, CDNs, or\\n  network calls.\\n- **Uncensored by design.** Do not \\"clean up\\" dictionary profanity — that\\n  defeats the project\'s purpose (and is explicitly out of scope in\\n  `SECURITY.md`).\\n- **Edit sources, rebuild.** Never hand-edit `index.html`. Run `node build.js`\\n  after source changes and commit the rebuilt file.\\n- **Render output as text, never `innerHTML`**, to avoid XSS from\\n  translated strings.\\n\\nAll contributors agree to abide by the [Code of Conduct](CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md)\\n(Contributor Covenant v2.1).\\n\\n## Security\\n\\nSee [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md) for the vulnerability reporting policy. In\\nshort: report privately, not via a public issue. Note that **profanity in the\\ndictionaries is explicitly out of scope** — it is an intended feature, not a\\nsecurity issue.\\n\\nSecurity-relevant properties of the codebase: no `innerHTML` of untrusted\\ntranslation output, no `eval` of user input, no network calls at runtime, no\\nexternal dependencies.\\n\\n## License\\n\\nThis project is licensed under the **MIT License** — see [LICENSE](LICENSE).\\n\\nYou are free to use, copy, modify, merge, publish, distribute, sublicense, and\\nsell copies of the software. The only requirement is that the copyright notice\\nand permission notice be included in all copies or substantial portions of the\\nsoftware. The software is provided \\"as is\\", without warranty of any kind.\\n", "file_path": "README.md"}'}}]}

See [README.md → The Honest Tradeoff](README.md#the-honest-tradeoff).\\n\\n### \\"It translated my swear word!\\"\\n\\nYes — that is the entire point. {'role': 'assistant', 'content': 'Now usage.md and CHANGELOG.md:', 'tool_calls': [{'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-92d7f7e9dc4b0749', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "# Usage\\n\\nA practical guide to using Uncensored Translator. For installation, see\\n[README.md → Quick Start](README.md#quick-start). For how the engine works\\ninternally, see [README.md → How It Works](README.md#how-it-works) and the\\n[wiki](https://github.com/uncensored-translator/uncensored-translator/wiki).\\n\\n## Opening the tool\\n\\nYou need the single file `index.html` and a modern browser. Three ways to open\\nit:\\n\\n### 1. Open the file directly\\n\\nDouble-click `index.html`, or:\\n\\n```bash\\n# macOS\\nopen index.html\\n\\n# Linux (xdg)\\nxdg-open index.html\\n```\\n\\nThis works because the project makes no network requests, so `file://` is fine.\\n\\n### 2. Serve it locally (recommended for development)\\n\\nSome browsers restrict `file://` in edge cases. A static server avoids that:\\n\\n```bash\\npython3 -m http.server 8000\\n# open http://localhost:8000/index.html\\n```\\n\\nor\\n\\n```bash\\nnpx --yes serve -l 8000\\n```\\n\\n### 3. Host it anywhere\\n\\nDrop `index.html` on GitHub Pages, Netlify, Vercel, S3, or any static host.\\nIt is fully self-contained — no build step needed to deploy, no environment\\nvariables, no backend.\\n\\n## The interface\\n\\nWhen you open `index.html` you see the terminal-styled UI:\\n\\n```\\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\\n│  UNCENSORED TRANSLATOR      SIGNAL LIVE  ENGINE LOCAL  NET OFF │\\n├───────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┤\\n│   SOURCE  [ ENGLISH ▾]│  TARGET  [ SPANISH ▾]                  │\\n│                       │                                        │\\n│  type here...         │   translation appears here...          │\\n│                       │                                        │\\n│  0 / 2000 CHARS       │                          [ CLEAR ] [ TRANSMIT ] │\\n└───────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────┘\\n                       [ ⇄ swap ]\\n```\\n\\n- **Left panel** — the source language and input box. Defaults to English.\\n- **Right panel** — the target language and output box. Defaults to Spanish.\\n- **Swap button** (⇄) — flips source/target languages **and** moves the output\\n  into the input box so you can translate it back (a round-trip).\\n- **CLEAR** — empties the input.\\n- **TRANSMIT** — manually triggers a translation (you can also press\\n  `Ctrl`/`Cmd`+`Enter`).\\n- **Header status** — `SIGNAL LIVE`, `ENGINE LOCAL`, `NET OFF`, and signal\\n  bars confirm the engine is running entirely on your machine.\\n\\n## Selecting languages\\n\\nEach panel has a dropdown with the 10 supported languages:\\n\\n| Code | Language    |\\n|------|-------------|\\n| `en` | English     |\\n| `zh` | Mandarin    |\\n| `hi` | Hindi       |\\n| `es` | Spanish     |\\n| `fr` | French      |\\n| `ar` | Arabic      |\\n| `bn` | Bengali     |\\n| `pt` | Portuguese  |\\n| `ru` | Russian     |\\n| `ur` | Urdu        |\\n\\nYou can set source and target to any combination, including the same language\\n(which returns the text unchanged).\\n\\n## Typing and translating\\n\\n1. Click the left input box and start typing.\\n2. As you type, the character counter updates: `N / 2000 CHARS`. The counter\\n   turns amber near the limit and red at 2,000.\\n3. After you stop typing for a brief moment (~420 ms debounce), the\\n   translation appears automatically in the right panel.\\n4. To force an immediate translation, press `Ctrl`+`Enter` (or `Cmd`+`Enter`\\n   on macOS), or click **TRANSMIT**.\\n\\n### Character limit\\n\\nThe input is capped at **2,000 characters**. This is a UX guard to keep the\\nphrase matcher responsive. For longer text, translate in chunks.\\n\\n## Keyboard shortcuts\\n\\n| Shortcut | Action |\\n|---|---|\\n| `Ctrl`/`Cmd` + `Enter` | Transmit (translate immediately) |\\n\\nMore shortcuts may be added; check the [CHANGELOG](CHANGELOG.md) for updates.\\n\\n## Swapping and round-tripping\\n\\nClick the **swap** button (⇄) between the panels. It does two things at once:\\n\\n1. Swaps the source and target languages.\\n2. Moves the current output into the input box.\\n\\nThis lets you immediately translate the result back into the original language\\n— a round-trip — which is useful for sanity-checking a translation or for\\nchaining through a third language.\\n\\n**Example round-trip:**\\n\\n```\\n1. en -> es :  \\"i love you\\"        =>  \\"te amo\\"\\n2. click swap (now es -> en, input = \\"te amo\\")\\n3. es -> en :  \\"te amo\\"            =>  \\"i love you\\"\\n```\\n\\n## Working with non-Latin scripts\\n\\nThe tokenizer handles Unicode combining marks (`\\\\p{M}`), so scripts with\\nvowel signs and viramas work correctly:\\n\\n- **Hindi (Devanagari):** `नमस्ते` is treated as one token, not split at the\\n  virama.\\n- **Bengali:** `ধন্যবাদ` is treated as one token.\\n- **Arabic:** `شكرا` is treated as one token.\\n- **Mandarin / Russian:** treated as expected (no combining-mark issues).\\n\\nYou can paste text in any of these scripts directly into the input box.\\n\\n## What translates and what doesn\'t\\n\\n- **In the dictionary** → translated, including multi-word phrases (matched\\n  longest-first). e.g. `good morning` → `buenos días` (one phrase), not\\n  `bueno` + `mañana`.\\n- **Not in the dictionary** → passed through unchanged. e.g.\\n  `supercalifragilistic` → `supercalifragilistic`.\\n- **Pivot pairs** (`X → Y` where neither is English) → translated in two steps\\n  via English. Both steps use phrase matching, but the English intermediate can\\n  lose nuance.\\n\\n## Uncensored output\\n\\nThe dictionaries include profanity, vulgarity, and slang in every language on\\npurpose. If you type a swear word that is in the dictionary, it will be\\ntranslated — not refused, not softened. This is the core feature. If that is\\nnot what you want, this is the wrong tool.\\n\\nSee [README.md → Why](README.md#why) and\\n[README.md → The Honest Tradeoff](README.md#the-honest-tradeoff).\\n\\n## Using the engine programmatically\\n\\nThe engine is reusable outside the UI. It is exposed as\\n`window.TRANSLATOR`.\\n\\n### In the browser\\n\\n```js\\nconst { translate, LANGS } = window.TRANSLATOR;\\n\\ntranslate(\\"i love you\\", \\"en\\", \\"es\\");   // \\"te amo\\"\\ntranslate(\\"te amo\\",     \\"es\\", \\"fr\\");   // \\"je t\'aime\\"  (pivot via English)\\ntranslate(\\"你好\\",       \\"zh\\", \\"en\\");   // \\"hello\\"\\ntranslate(\\"good morning\\",\\"en\\",\\"zh\\");   // \\"早上好\\"\\ntranslate(\\"fuck you\\",   \\"en\\", \\"ru\\");   // \\"пошёл на хуй\\"\\n\\nLANGS; // [\\"en\\",\\"zh\\",\\"hi\\",\\"es\\",\\"fr\\",\\"ar\\",\\"bn\\",\\"pt\\",\\"ru\\",\\"ur\\"]\\n```\\n\\n### In Node\\n\\n```js\\nglobal.window = {};\\nconst fs = require(\\"fs\\");\\nconst code = [\\"dict_part1.js\\", \\"dict_part2.js\\", \\"engine.js\\"]\\n  .map(f => fs.readFileSync(f, \\"utf8\\")).join(\\"\\\\n;\\\\n\\");\\neval(code);\\n\\nconst { translate, LANGS } = global.window.TRANSLATOR;\\ntranslate(\\"i am hungry\\", \\"en\\", \\"ru\\");  // \\"я голоден\\"\\n```\\n\\n### API reference\\n\\n```ts\\ninterface Translator {\\n  translate(text: string, src: LangCode, tgt: LangCode): string;\\n  LANGS: LangCode[]; // [\\"en\\",\\"zh\\",\\"hi\\",\\"es\\",\\"fr\\",\\"ar\\",\\"bn\\",\\"pt\\",\\"ru\\",\\"ur\\"]\\n}\\n\\ntype LangCode = \\"en\\" | \\"zh\\" | \\"hi\\" | \\"es\\" | \\"fr\\" | \\"ar\\" | \\"bn\\" | \\"pt\\" | \\"ru\\" | \\"ur\\";\\n```\\n\\n- `translate` never throws on unknown input — unknown words pass through.\\n- `translate(text, x, x)` returns `text` unchanged.\\n- Source/target can be any of the 10 codes in any combination (90 directed\\n  pairs).\\n\\n## Troubleshooting\\n\\n| Symptom | Likely cause / fix |\\n|---|---|\\n| A word came through unchanged | Not in the dictionary — add it (see [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md)) |\\n| Word order looks wrong in output | Expected — no grammar reshuffling (see [README.md → Honest Tradeoff](README.md#the-honest-tradeoff)) |\\n| Page is blank / nothing happens | Try a static server instead of `file://`; check browser console |\\n| Character counter is red | You hit the 2,000-char cap — shorten the input |\\n| Translation didn\'t update | Wait for the debounce, or press `Ctrl`/`Cmd`+`Enter` |\\n\\nFor anything else, see [SUPPORT.md](SUPPORT.md).\\n", "file_path": "usage.md"}'}}, {'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-beb06b9b55bd124a', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "# Changelog\\n\\nAll notable changes to Uncensored Translator are documented in this file.\\n\\nThe format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),\\nand this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).\\n\\n## [Unreleased]\\n\\n### Added\\n- GitHub issue templates: `bug_report.md`, `feature_request.md`, and\\n  `config.yml` (issue chooser with links to Discussions and private security\\n  reporting).\\n- GitHub discussion template (`.github/DISCUSSION_TEMPLATE/general.yml`).\\n- Community docs: `SUPPORT.md`, `usage.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`,\\n  `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` (Contributor Covenant v2.1), `SECURITY.md`.\\n- Assembly script `build.js` (deterministic build of `index.html` from sources).\\n- `screenshots/` directory with `clean-translation.png` and\\n  `uncensored-proof.png` for the README.\\n- `wiki/` content: Home, Architecture, Adding-Dictionary-Entries,\\n  Language-Support, and a `_Sidebar.md`.\\n- Social preview image (`assets/social-preview.png`).\\n- `.gitignore`.\\n\\n### Changed\\n- README expanded with full How-It-Works section, verified examples,\\n  screenshots, and cross-links to all community files.\\n\\n### Notes\\n- No behavioral change to the engine or dictionaries in this batch — it is\\n  documentation, templates, and project plumbing only. `node build.js` still\\n  produces a byte-identical `index.html`; the engine translates whatever you type rather\\n  than refusing or softening it.\\n- **Terminal \\"raw signal / wire transmission\\" UI**: monospace, scanline\\n  overlay, grid background, glowing wire seam, signal-strength bars.\\n- **Auto-translate** with ~420 ms debounce; English source → `fwd[tgt]`; English\\n  target → `rev[src]`; otherwise two-step pivot.\\n- Unknown tokens pass through unchanged (engine never throws on unknown input).\\n\\n### Known limitations (documented, not bugs)\\n- No grammar or word-order reshuffling (phrase substitution, not a neural\\n  model).\\n- No context / disambiguation — one sense per word.\\n- Finite coverage (~2,500 entries); unknown words pass through.\\n- Pivot pairs can be rougher than direct pairs due to the English intermediate.\\n\\n### Security properties (by design)\\n- No `innerHTML` of untrusted translation output (rendered as text nodes).\\n- No `eval` of user input.\\n- No network calls at runtime.\\n- No external dependencies.\\n", "file_path": "CHANGELOG.md"}'}}]}

This is the deeper documentation\\nbehind the [README](https://github.com/uncensored-translator/uncensored-translator#readme).\\n\\nUncensored Translator is a self-contained, offline, **uncensored** translator\\nfor the top 10 languages by total speakers. The entire engine — dictionaries\\nand all — runs in your browser with no servers, no APIs, and no network calls.\\n\\n## Pages\\n\\n- **[[Architecture]]** — how the engine, dictionaries, build, and UI fit\\n  together.\\n- **[[Adding-Dictionary-Entries]]** — the entry format, conventions, and how to\\n  add words/phrases across all 9 dictionaries.\\n- **[[Language-Support]]** — the 10 languages, why they were chosen, and\\n  script/tokenization notes (especially the `\\\\p{M}` combining-mark handling for\\n  Devanagari, Bengali, and Arabic).\\n\\n## Start here\\n\\n- New to the project? One file.\\n4. **Honest.** The docs and in-app footer are explicit that this is phrase\\n   substitution, not a neural model — grammar and word order are not\\n   reshuffled.\\n\\nAnything that conflicts with these four is, by definition, out of scope.\\n", "file_path": "wiki/Home.md"}'}}, {'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-937ca5d2813a934d', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "# Architecture\\n\\nThis page describes how the pieces of Uncensored Translator fit together.\\n\\n## High-level\\n\\n```\\n            ┌─────────────── SOURCE FILES ───────────────┐\\n            │                                            │\\n            │  ui.html   (template, 2 injection markers) │\\n            │  styles.css                                │\\n            │  dict_part1.js  (EN-ZH, EN-HI, EN-ES, EN-FR)│\\n            │  dict_part2.js  (EN-AR, EN-BN, EN-PT, EN-RU, EN-UR)│\\n            │  engine.js                                 │\\n            │  ui.js                                     │\\n            └───────────────────┬────────────────────────┘\\n                                │  node build.js\\n                                ▼\\n            ┌─────────── ASSEMBLED OUTPUT ───────────────┐\\n            │           index.html  (~110 KB)            │\\n            │   <style>  ← styles.css inlined            │\\n            │   <script> ← dicts + engine + ui inlined   │\\n            └───────────────────┬────────────────────────┘\\n                                │  open in browser\\n                                ▼\\n                     runs fully offline\\n```\\n\\nThe shipped artifact is a single `index.html`. It is **assembled** from source\\nfiles by `build.js` so the sources stay maintainable, but the output is\\ncommitted to the repo so it is usable without Node.\\n\\n## Files\\n\\n| File | Role |\\n|---|---|\\n| `index.html` | Assembled, self-contained, shippable file. Do not hand-edit. |\\n| `build.js` | Assembly script. Deterministic — reproduces byte-identical output. |\\n| `ui.html` | HTML template containing two markers: `/* CSS injected here... */` and `/* JS injected here... */`. |\\n| `styles.css` | All CSS. The \\"raw signal / wire transmission\\" terminal aesthetic. |\\n| `dict_part1.js` | Dictionaries `DICT_EN_ZH`, `DICT_EN_HI`, `DICT_EN_ES`, `DICT_EN_FR` (~1,250 entries). |\\n| `dict_part2.js` | Dictionaries `DICT_EN_AR`, `DICT_EN_BN`, `DICT_EN_PT`, `DICT_EN_RU`, `DICT_EN_UR` (~1,250 entries). |\\n| `engine.js` | Tokenizer + `translateDirect` + `translate` (pivot router). Exposes `window.TRANSLATOR`. |\\n| `ui.js` | UI logic: dropdowns, auto-translate, swap, transmit, char counter. |\\n| `test_engine.js` | Node harness; evals dicts+engine and prints test cases. |\\n\\n## The engine (`engine.js`)\\n\\n### Data structures\\n\\nAt load time, each bilingual dictionary `DICT_EN_XX` is compiled into two maps:\\n\\n```js\\nfwd[xx][englishWord] = xxWord;   // English -> other language\\nrev[xx][xxWord]      = englishWord; // other language -> English\\n```\\n\\nSo one dictionary entry `{ en: \\"hello\\", es: \\"hola\\" }` produces both\\n`fwd.es[\\"hello\\"] = \\"hola\\"` and `rev.es[\\"hola\\"] = \\"hello\\"`.\\n\\n### Tokenizer\\n\\n```js\\nconst wordRe = /[\\\\p{L}\\\\p{N}\\\\p{M}]+(?:[\'\'\\\\-\\\\][\\\\p{L}\\\\p{N}\\\\p{M}]+)*/gu;\\n```\\n\\n- `\\\\p{L}` — letters\\n- `\\\\p{N}` — numbers\\n- `\\\\p{M}` — **combining marks** (virama, vowel signs, diacritics). This is the\\n  critical class. Without it, Devanagari `नमस्ते` gets split into\\n  `नमस` + `्` + `त` + `े` and never matches. Including `\\\\p{M}` keeps each\\n  script\'s combined grapheme cluster as one token.\\n- The optional tail `(?:[\'\'\\\\-\\\\][\\\\p{L}\\\\p{N}\\\\p{M}]+)*` keeps apostrophe/hyphen-\\n  joined words together (`don\'t`, `well-being`).\\n\\nNon-word characters (spaces, punctuation) are preserved as \\"gap\\" tokens so the\\noutput retains the input\'s punctuation and spacing.\\n\\n### `translateDirect(text, table)`\\n\\nSingle-step translation against one lookup table:\\n\\n1. Tokenize the input.\\n2. Walk the token list. At each position, try to match the **longest** phrase\\n   (up to 6 word tokens ahead, skipping non-word gaps) against the table.\\n3. On a hit, emit the translation and advance past the matched phrase.\\n4. On a miss, emit the original token unchanged and advance by one.\\n\\nLongest-first means `good morning` (a 2-word entry) wins over `good` + `morning`\\n(two 1-word entries matched separately).\\n\\n### `translate(text, src, tgt)`\\n\\n```js\\nfunction translate(text, src, tgt) {\\n  if (src === tgt) return text;\\n  if (src === \\"en\\") return translateDirect(text, fwd[tgt]);\\n  if (tgt === \\"en\\") return translateDirect(text, rev[src]);\\n  // Two-step pivot: src -> en -> tgt. Both legs use full phrase matching.\\n  const enIntermediate = translateDirect(text, rev[src]);\\n  return translateDirect(enIntermediate, fwd[tgt]);\\n}\\n```\\n\\nThe pivot is the key to covering 90 pairs from 9 dictionaries: any `X → Y`\\ntranslation goes `X → English → Y`, and **both legs** call `translateDirect`,\\nso both legs get full longest-phrase-first matching. (An earlier version did\\nthe second leg word-by-word; fixing it to use `translateDirect` on both legs\\nwas a significant correctness improvement.)\\n\\n### Export\\n\\n```js\\nwindow.TRANSLATOR = { translate, LANGS };\\n```\\n\\n`LANGS = [\\"en\\",\\"zh\\",\\"hi\\",\\"es\\",\\"fr\\",\\"ar\\",\\"bn\\",\\"pt\\",\\"ru\\",\\"ur\\"]`.\\n\\n## The UI (`ui.js`)\\n\\n- `LANG_META` — array of `{ code, name, tag }` for the 10 languages; populates\\n  both dropdowns.\\n- Default: source `en`, target `es`.\\n- `MAX = 2000` — input cap; counter turns amber at 80%, red at 100%.\\n- Auto-translate: debounced (~420 ms) on input.\\n- Manual transmit: `Ctrl`/`Cmd`+`Enter` or the TRANSMIT button → triggers the\\n  wire-pulse animation + signal-bar flash.\\n- Swap button: flips `src`/`tgt` **and** moves the output text into the source\\n  box (round-trip).\\n- **Output is rendered as text nodes, never `innerHTML`** — translated strings\\n  may contain `<`, `>`, `&`, so injecting as HTML would be an XSS vector.\\n\\n## The build (`build.js`)\\n\\n1. Read `ui.html`.\\n2. Read `styles.css` → inject into the CSS marker.\\n3. Read `dict_part1.js + dict_part2.js + engine.js + ui.js`, join with `\\\\n;\\\\n`\\n   → inject into the JS marker.\\n4. Write `index.html`.\\n\\nDeterministic: identical inputs → byte-identical output. Verified by\\ndiffing a rebuilt file against the committed one.\\n\\n## The test harness (`test_engine.js`)\\n\\n```js\\nglobal.window = {};\\nconst code = [\\"dict_part1.js\\", \\"dict_part2.js\\", \\"engine.js\\"]\\n  .map(f => fs.readFileSync(f, \\"utf8\\")).join(\\"\\\\n;\\\\n\\");\\neval(code);\\nconst TR = global.window.TRANSLATOR;\\n```\\n\\nThen prints translations for direct pairs, pivot pairs, profanity, and\\npassthrough. No test framework — read the output by eye. Add cases as you add\\nentries.\\n\\n## Why this architecture\\n\\n- **Single file** because the goal is \\"download and run, anywhere, offline.\\"\\n- **Pivot through English** because 9 bilingual dictionaries (2504 entries) are\\n  far cheaper to build and maintain than 45 direct pairwise dictionaries\\n  (~12,500 entries) for the same 90-pair coverage.\\n- **Longest-phrase-first** because naive word-by-word matching produces\\n  `\\"bueno mañana\\"` instead of `\\"buenos días\\"` for `good morning`.\\n- **`\\\\p{M}` in the tokenizer** because Indic and Arabic scripts are\\n  combining-mark-heavy; omitting it silently breaks those languages.\\n", "file_path": "wiki/Architecture.md"}'}}]}

{'role': 'assistant', 'content': 'Now the remaining wiki pages:', 'tool_calls': [{'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-af10138572613a45', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "# Adding Dictionary Entries\\n\\nThis page is the detailed companion to the \\"Dictionary entries\\" section of\\n[CONTRIBUTING.md](https://github.com/uncensored-translator/uncensored-translator/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md).\\nIf you want to improve translation coverage, this is where you start.\\n\\n## The entry format\\n\\nEvery entry is a plain object with an `en` key and one other-language key\\n(two-letter code):\\n\\n```js\\n{ en: \\"hello\\", es: \\"hola\\" }\\n```\\n\\nThat single line automatically produces **both** directions:\\n\\n- `fwd.es[\\"hello\\"] = \\"hola\\"` (English → Spanish)\\n- `rev.es[\\"hola\\"] = \\"hello\\"` (Spanish → English)\\n\\nYou never write the reverse entry yourself.\\n\\n## Where entries live\\n\\n- `dict_part1.js` — `DICT_EN_ZH`, `DICT_EN_HI`, `DICT_EN_ES`, `DICT_EN_FR`\\n- `dict_part2.js` — `DICT_EN_AR`, `DICT_EN_BN`, `DICT_EN_PT`, `DICT_EN_RU`,\\n  `DICT_EN_UR`\\n\\nEach is a top-level `const DICT_EN_XX = [ ... ];` array. Add entries inside the\\narray, ideally grouped by category with a comment, matching the existing\\nstyle:\\n\\n```js\\n // food\\n { en: \\"bread\\", es: \\"pan\\" },\\n { en: \\"water\\", es: \\"agua\\" },\\n { en: \\"coffee\\", es: \\"café\\" },\\n```\\n\\n## Conventions\\n\\n### Lowercase keys\\n\\nThe engine lowercases input before lookup. **Store keys in lowercase.** Do not\\nstore `\\"Hello\\"` — store `\\"hello\\"`. Capitalization in the user\'s input is\\nignored, and output case is not currently transformed.\\n\\n### Preserve diacritics — do not fold them\\n\\nMatch the natural spelling: `cómo`, `café`, `naïve`, `sobremesa`. The\\ntokenizer preserves combining marks, so diacritics are part of the token. Do\\nnot strip them to \\"simplify\\" matching — that would break correctness for\\nlanguages where diacritics are phonemically distinct.\\n\\n### Prefer phrases over single words when a phrase exists\\n\\nThe engine matches **longest-first**. So `{ en: \\"good morning\\", es: \\"buenos días\\" }`\\nas one entry wins over the two separate entries `{ en: \\"good\\" }` +\\n`{ en: \\"morning\\" }` matched individually. Add the phrase as a single entry.\\n\\nBut **also keep the component words** as individual entries, so that when only\\npart of a phrase is present (`\\"good evening\\"` when only `\\"good morning\\"` is\\ndefined), the words still translate.\\n\\n### Aim for consistent cross-language coverage\\n\\nA word added to one dictionary will only translate to/from that one language.\\nFor good **pivot** behavior (`X → English → Y`), the same English word should\\nideally exist in all 9 dictionaries. Inconsistent coverage does not crash —\\nthe word just passes through unchanged in the missing direction — but\\nconsistency makes pivots much more useful.\\n\\nIf you only know a subset of languages, contribute what you know. Partial\\ncoverage is welcome; reviewers may fill in the rest.\\n\\n### Profanity is in scope\\n\\nUncensored by design. Profanity, vulgarity, and slang belong in the\\ndictionaries. Do not self-censor, and do not \\"clean up\\" existing entries. See\\n[SECURITY.md](https://github.com/uncensored-translator/uncensored-translator/blob/main/SECURITY.md):\\nprofanity is explicitly out of scope for reports.\\n\\n## A worked example: adding \\"coffee\\" across all 9 dictionaries\\n\\n`dict_part1.js`:\\n\\n```js\\n // drinks\\n { en: \\"coffee\\", zh: \\"咖啡\\" },\\n { en: \\"coffee\\", hi: \\"कॉफी\\" },\\n { en: \\"coffee\\", es: \\"café\\" },\\n { en: \\"coffee\\", fr: \\"café\\" },\\n```\\n\\n`dict_part2.js`:\\n\\n```js\\n // drinks\\n { en: \\"coffee\\", ar: \\"قهوة\\" },\\n { en: \\"coffee\\", bn: \\"কফি\\" },\\n { en: \\"coffee\\", pt: \\"café\\" },\\n { en: \\"coffee\\", ru: \\"кофе\\" },\\n { en: \\"coffee\\", ur: \\"کافی\\" },\\n```\\n\\nNow `coffee` translates in all 90 directions that pass through it, e.g.\\n`es → ru` (\\"café\\" → English \\"coffee\\" → \\"кофе\\").\\n\\n## After adding entries\\n\\n1. **Rebuild:**\\n   ```bash\\n   node build.js\\n   ```\\n2. **Add a test case** to `test_engine.js`:\\n   ```js\\n   t(\\"en\\", \\"es\\", \\"coffee\\");\\n   t(\\"es\\", \\"ru\\", \\"café\\");   // pivot\\n   ```\\n3. **Run tests:**\\n   ```bash\\n   node test_engine.js\\n   ```\\n   Confirm the expected output prints.\\n4. **Commit** the source files **and** the rebuilt `index.html` together.\\n\\n## Things to avoid\\n\\n- **Do not** store capitalized keys (`\\"Hello\\"`).\\n- **Do not** fold diacritics (`\\"cafe\\"` instead of `\\"café\\"`).\\n- **Do not** duplicate the reverse direction — one entry covers both.\\n- **Do not** filter or soften profanity.\\n- **Do not** hand-edit `index.html` — edit the source and rebuild.\\n- **Do not** add entries that require network lookups. The dictionaries are\\n  static data.\\n", "file_path": "wiki/Adding-Dictionary-Entries.md"}'}}, {'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-848d25f9491971c8', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "# Language Support\\n\\n## The 10 languages\\n\\nUncensored Translator supports the top 10 languages by **total speakers**\\n(native + second-language speakers), per widely-cited rankings:\\n\\n| Code | Language    | Script       | Family              |\\n|------|-------------|--------------|---------------------|\\n| `en` | English     | Latin        | Indo-European (Germanic) |\\n| `zh` | Mandarin    | Han (Simplified) | Sino-Tibetan   |\\n| `hi` | Hindi       | Devanagari   | Indo-European (Indic)    |\\n| `es` | Spanish     | Latin        | Indo-European (Romance)  |\\n| `fr` | French      | Latin        | Indo-European (Romance)  |\\n| `ar` | Arabic      | Arabic       | Afro-Asiatic (Semitic)   |\\n| `bn` | Bengali     | Bengali      | Indo-European (Indic)    |\\n| `pt` | Portuguese  | Latin        | Indo-European (Romance)  |\\n| `ru` | Russian     | Cyrillic     | Indo-European (Slavic)   |\\n| `ur` | Urdu        | Arabic (Nastaliq) | Indo-European (Indic) |\\n\\n## Why these 10?\\n\\nThey are the languages with the largest total speaker populations. Choosing\\nthe top 10 by total speakers (rather than native speakers alone) keeps the\\ntool useful for the broadest set of real-world conversations, since many of\\nthese are major second languages.\\n\\nThe 10-language scope is a **deliberate design constraint**, not an oversight.\\nAdding an 11th language changes the project\'s framing. Feature requests for\\nadditional languages are considered, but the boundary is intentional. See\\n[SUPPORT.md](https://github.com/uncensored-translator/uncensored-translator/blob/main/SUPPORT.md)\\n→ \\"I want an 11th language.\\"\\n\\n## Why English is the pivot\\n\\nEnglish is the pivot language for two reasons:\\n\\n1. **Coverage efficiency.** 9 bilingual dictionaries (English ↔ each other\\n   language) yield all 90 directed pairs. Building 45 direct pairwise\\n   dictionaries would require ~12,500 entries for the same coverage — 5× the\\n   work.\\n2. **English is a common second language** among the speaker populations of\\n   the other 9, so an English intermediate is often a meaningful bridge.\\n\\nThe tradeoff: a two-step `X → English → Y` pivot can lose nuance that a direct\\n`X → Y` dictionary would preserve, because the English word is the bottleneck.\\nThis is documented in the README\'s \\"Honest Tradeoff\\" section.\\n\\n## Script and tokenization notes\\n\\nThe tokenizer is `/[\\\\p{L}\\\\p{N}\\\\p{M}]+(?:[\'\'\\\\-\\\\][\\\\p{L}\\\\p{N}\\\\p{M}]+)*/gu`. The\\n`\\\\p{M}` (combining marks) class is essential for several of these languages:\\n\\n### Hindi (Devanagari) — `hi`\\n\\nDevanagari uses a virama (U+094D) to suppress inherent vowels and vowel signs\\n(U+093E–U+094C) that combine with consonants. These are `\\\\p{M}` marks, **not**\\n`\\\\p{L}` letters. Without `\\\\p{M}` in the tokenizer, a word like `नमस्ते`\\n(\\"namaste\\") gets split into `नमस` + `्` + `त` + `े` and never matches the\\ndictionary. Including `\\\\p{M}` keeps the whole cluster as one token.\\n\\n### Bengali — `bn`\\n\\nSame situation as Hindi: Bengali vowel signs and the virama (U+09CD) are\\ncombining marks. `ধন্যবাদ` (\\"thank you\\") is one token only with `\\\\p{M}`\\nincluded.\\n\\n### Arabic — `ar`\\n\\nArabic diacritics (harakat) are combining marks. Even without full\\nvocalization, including `\\\\p{M}` future-proofs matching for vocalized text.\\nThe dictionary stores unvocalized forms (e.g. `شكرا`); input with or without\\ndiacritics is handled by the tokenizer\'s grapheme clustering.\\n\\n### Urdu — `ur`\\n\\nUrdu is written in a Nastaliq style of the Arabic script, with extensive\\ncombining marks and ligatures. The same `\\\\p{M}` handling applies. Note that\\nNastaliq rendering can be visually complex, but the underlying Unicode\\ncodepoints tokenize cleanly.\\n\\n### Mandarin — `zh`\\n\\nSimplified Han characters. No combining marks needed; `\\\\p{L}` covers Han.\\nEntries store simplified forms (e.g. `你好`, `早上好`). The dictionary does not\\ncurrently distinguish Traditional vs Simplified — it is Simplified by default.\\n\\n### Russian — `ru`\\n\\nCyrillic. Covered by `\\\\p{L}`. No special handling needed. Case is lowercased\\nbefore lookup (Russian has capitalization rules, but the engine lowercases\\nall input uniformly).\\n\\n### Spanish, French, Portuguese — `es`, `fr`, `pt`\\n\\nLatin script with diacritics. The diacritics in these languages are sometimes\\nprecomposed (single codepoint, e.g. `é` = U+00E9) and sometimes decomposed\\n(base + combining mark). `\\\\p{M}` ensures both forms tokenize consistently.\\n**Diacritics are preserved in dictionary keys** — do not fold them.\\n\\n## Coverage status\\n\\nEach of the 9 bilingual dictionaries has ~250–300 entries spanning greetings,\\npronouns, question words, common verbs (with tense variants), nouns,\\nadjectives, adverbs, connectors, numbers, common phrases, and an\\nintentionally-included profanity/vulgarity set. Total: ~2,500 entries.\\n\\nCoverage is finite and uneven across domains. Unknown words pass through\\nunchanged. The fastest way to improve a specific translation is to add the\\nmissing entry — see\\n[[Adding-Dictionary-Entries]].\\n\\n## Pair matrix\\n\\nWith 10 languages and English as pivot, all 90 directed pairs are covered:\\n\\n```\\n       en  zh  hi  es  fr  ar  bn  pt  ru  ur\\n  en    -   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓\\n  zh    ✓   -   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓\\n  hi    ✓   ✓   -   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓\\n  es    ✓   ✓   ✓   -   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓\\n  fr    ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   -   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓\\n  ar    ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   -   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓\\n  bn    ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   -   ✓   ✓   ✓\\n  pt    ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   -   ✓   ✓\\n  ru    ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   -   ✓\\n  ur    ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   ✓   -\\n```\\n\\nA `✓` means the pair translates via either a direct dictionary lookup (when\\nEnglish is source or target) or a two-step pivot (otherwise).\\n", "file_path": "wiki/Language-Support.md"}'}}, {'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-950e7f5ceb05b9f7', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "# Uncensored Translator Wiki\\n\\n- **[[Home]]**\\n- **[[Architecture]]**\\n- **[[Adding-Dictionary-Entries]]**\\n- **[[Language-Support]]**\\n\\n---\\n\\n[📖 README](https://github.com/uncensored-translator/uncensored-translator#readme) ·\\n[🛠️ CONTRIBUTING](https://github.com/uncensored-translator/uncensored-translator/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md) ·\\n[💬 Discussions](https://github.com/uncensored-translator/uncensored-translator/discussions) ·\\n[🔒 Security](https://github.com/uncensored-translator/uncensored-translator/blob/main/SECURITY.md)\\n", "file_path": "wiki/_Sidebar.md"}'}}]}

{'role': 'assistant', 'content': 'Now the store (persistence layer with localStorage):', 'tool_calls': [{'id': 'chatcmpl-tool-b84523bdf6ecb8b1', 'type': 'function', 'function': {'name': 'create_file', 'arguments': '{"file_contents": "// ============================================================\\n// BLOCKTRUST — STORE (state + localStorage persistence)\\n// ============================================================\\n// A tiny reactive store. State lives in memory; changes are\\n// persisted to localStorage so posts/likes/comments/profile\\n// survive refreshes. A simple pub/sub lets the UI re-render.\\n\\nconst STORE_KEY = \\"blocktrust_v2\\";\\n\\nconst Store = (() => {\\n  let state = null;\\n  const subs = new Set();\\n\\n  function defaultState() {\\n    return {\\n      version: 2,\\n      me: { ...DEFAULT_ME },\\n      users: structuredCloneSafe(SEED_USERS),\\n      posts: structuredCloneSafe(SEED_POSTS),\\n      alerts: [],            // {id, postId, type, text, read, createdAt}\\n      theme: \\"light\\",        // light | dark\\n      premium: false,\\n      draft: \\"\\",             // saved composer draft\\n      activeCategory: \\"all\\",\\n      sort: \\"recent\\",        // recent | top | urgent\\n      lastSeen: Date.now(),\\n    };\\n  }\\n\\n  function structuredCloneSafe(obj) {\\n    return JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(obj));\\n  }\\n\\n  function load() {\\n    try {\\n      const raw = localStorage.getItem(STORE_KEY);\\n      if (!raw) return defaultState();\\n      const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);\\n      // Merge with defaults to handle new fields on upgrade\\n      const base = defaultState();\\n      return { ...base, ...parsed, me: { ...base.me, ...(parsed.me || {}) } };\\n    } catch (e) {\\n      console.warn(\\"Store load failed, using defaults:\\", e);\\n      return defaultState();\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  function save() {\\n    try {\\n      localStorage.setItem(STORE_KEY, JSON.stringify(state));\\n    } catch (e) {\\n      // Most likely quota (base64 images). Try saving without images.\\n      console.warn(\\"Store save failed (likely quota):\\", e);\\n      Toast.show(\\"Storage full — images may not persist.\\", \\"warn\\");\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  function init() {\\n    state = load();\\n    // generate any missing alert timestamps\\n    notify();\\n  }\\n\\n  function getState() { return state; }\\n\\n  function update(mutator) {\\n    mutator(state);\\n    save();\\n    notify();\\n  }\\n\\n  function subscribe(fn) {\\n    subs.add(fn);\\n    return () => subs.delete(fn);\\n  }\\n\\n  function notify() {\\n    subs.forEach(fn => { try { fn(state); } catch (e) { console.error(e); } });\\n  }\\n\\n  // ---- Domain actions ----\\n\\n  function addPost({ title, content, category, location, tags, boosted, reward, image }) {\\n    const post = {\\n      id: \\"p\\" + Date.now(),\\n      userId: \\"me\\",\\n      category: category || \\"news\\",\\n      title: title || \\"\\",\\n      content: content || \\"\\",\\n      location: location || state.me.neighborhood,\\n      tags: (tags || []).filter(Boolean),\\n      boosted: !!boosted,\\n      urgent: category === \\"urgent\\",\\n      reward: Math.max(0, parseInt(reward) || 0),\\n      rewardStatus: (parseInt(reward) || 0) > 0 ?
